# Rounds 7, 8 and 9 Experimental evidence archive

This note preserves the manifested, redacted evidence reviewed for the 2026-08-17
RFC-0088 seventh, eighth and ninth Experimental runs. The filename says `round7`
because the RFC and the figure verifier key off it.

**It carries three kinds of member.** The fixture sources and results for the
architectural spikes (`s1/`, `s2/`, `s3/`); the tooling that gates and checks them
(`build-archive.py`, `verify-note-figures-r7.py`, the runners); and **round 9's
apparatus measurement** — `r9-mutation-harness.py`, `r9-claim-accounting.py`, their
two helper scripts, and the three `s9/` result files. The `s9/` members are tool
output rather than fixture runs, so they carry no browser, uid or per-run nonce and
are exempted from the provenance and failing-row gates; every other results member
is subject to both. An earlier build of this archive contained none of the round-9
members, which left that round's headline figure resting on evidence no
reconstruction could produce. **Every results file it carries was produced by the fixture sources it ships**,
and every one carries a provenance block naming the platform, uid and per-run
nonce it was produced on. Four build gates enforce this, and each exists because
the defect it catches actually shipped: two results files sharing a digest are
refused; a results file with no provenance block is refused; a deferral or
missing-artifact marker sitting at a member path is refused; and an import a
manifested driver needs that is not itself a member is refused. `run-linux-r7.sh`
additionally refuses to copy a container artifact back unless that artifact's own
provenance says `linux`. It excludes third-party package
trees, browser binaries and profiles, TLS private key material, downloads, and
every other transient runtime file.

- Archive media type: `application/gzip` containing a tar archive
- Archive size: 192,717 bytes
- Archive SHA-256:
  `29fb063caf5343aa0d56ce3a9844fb3fecbe5682d0b79fa4a125f9882ee0ca1f`
- Members: 87 bounded regular files: the manifest plus the
  86 manifested files
- Uncompressed member bytes: 952,564
- Largest member: 142,846 bytes

The archive is evidence support, not a production deliverable. Its executable
fixture sources are inert until a reviewer deliberately reconstructs and runs
them in an approved temporary path. No third-party package tree, browser
payload, profile, cookie store, credential value, TLS private key, or download
is included.

Every promoted observable passes a redaction step, and `build-archive.py` —
itself a manifested member, so the gate is auditable — enforces it. The build
fails on any home directory, path-mangled account name, per-user temporary
path, uid-bearing temporary root, hostname, or key material. Members that name
those strings as their own detection patterns carry a per-file exemption for
the bare literal only; a key marker followed by PEM body fails even there.

The method-policy fixture generates a throwaway TLS chain with `openssl` into a
temporary root **outside** the evidence tree and deletes it at teardown, so no
key material is reachable from this archive. The privacy gate fails closed on a
PEM body regardless of which member carries it, and it no longer exempts short
hostnames — an earlier length floor let any host named in three characters or
fewer through.

**Numeric uids are promoted deliberately, and the decision is recorded here
rather than left implicit.** Round 7's and round 8's provenance blocks carry a
`uid` field, and it is load-bearing: the Linux platform rows claim to have been
measured as an unprivileged user, and `PARENT-IS-UNPRIVILEGED` is now an asserted
row rather than a recorded field. **All three Linux artifacts in this archive
report uid 1001** — including the namespace boundary, which round 6 could only
measure as root. The confined child inside that arm reports uid 0, because
`unshare -r` creates a new *user* namespace and maps the invoking user to root
inside it; that uid is namespace-local, and reading it as "still running as root"
would invert the finding. Container uids are not a person's. The macOS blocks
carry uid 501, the default first-user id on essentially every single-user macOS
install — no name, no path, no handle, no directory identifier, and the privacy
rule this repo enforces targets those. If a future round needs to drop the field,
the macOS claims do not depend on it; the Linux ones do.

`verify-note-figures-r7.py` ships here too and runs unchanged from a
reconstruction. Two of its facts — the archive's own digest — cannot be
re-derived there, because the archive blob is not a member of itself; it reports
those by name under `figuresNotCheckableHere` rather than silently checking two
fewer things, which is what an earlier version did. The manifest-derived member
count is checkable from a reconstruction, and the digest itself is verified by
the procedure above before extraction. `run-all-r7.sh` and `run-linux-r7.sh` ship as well, so
the run order and the platform split are auditable rather than described.

**Where these figures come from, since a reader should be able to trace them.**
The member and manifest counts are derivable from `manifest-r7.sha256`, which *is*
a member. The **byte figures are not**: they come from the builder's
`archive-summary-r7.json`, which is deliberately not a member — a summary
describing the archive that contains it cannot state its own size without
circularity. What the reconstruction below *does* verify directly is the
**digest**, by hashing the embedded payload before extracting anything, so a
wrong size figure cannot survive alongside a matching digest. Round 9's
claim-accounting tool surfaced this: the byte figures were the only prose numbers
it could not trace to a promoted artifact.

Reconstruct and validate it with the procedure below. Validation completes
before any member is written: absolute paths, traversal, duplicates, links,
devices, special permission bits, and oversized archives or members fail
closed. Extraction uses a fresh temporary root and exclusive file creation.

```bash
set -euo pipefail
EVIDENCE_ROOT=$(mktemp -d /private/tmp/rfc0088-round7-evidence.XXXXXX)
[ -n "$EVIDENCE_ROOT" ] || exit 1
# Remove the extraction tree on SUCCESS only. A failed reconstruction is
# exactly when a reviewer needs the tree, so on a non-zero exit it is kept
# and its path printed. Without this, each run left ~700 KB behind.
trap 'rc=$?; if [ $rc -eq 0 ]; then rm -rf "$EVIDENCE_ROOT"; \
  else echo "reconstruction failed (exit $rc); tree kept at $EVIDENCE_ROOT"; fi' EXIT
python3 - "$EVIDENCE_ROOT/evidence.tgz" <<'PY'

import pathlib
import sys

"""
pathlib.Path(sys.argv[1]).write_bytes(base64.b64decode(payload))
PY
python3 - \
  "$EVIDENCE_ROOT/evidence.tgz" \
  "$EVIDENCE_ROOT" \
  "29fb063caf5343aa0d56ce3a9844fb3fecbe5682d0b79fa4a125f9882ee0ca1f" <<'PY'
import hashlib
import pathlib
import sys
import tarfile

archive = pathlib.Path(sys.argv[1])
root = pathlib.Path(sys.argv[2]).resolve(strict=True)
actual = hashlib.sha256(archive.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
if actual != sys.argv[3]:
    raise SystemExit(f"evidence archive digest mismatch: {actual}")

with tarfile.open(archive, "r:gz") as bundle:
    members = bundle.getmembers()
    if len(members) != 87:
        raise SystemExit("unexpected evidence member count")
    if len({member.name for member in members}) != len(members):
        raise SystemExit("evidence archive contains duplicate paths")
    if sum(member.size for member in members) > 2 * 1024 * 1024:
        raise SystemExit("evidence archive exceeds the total size limit")

    validated = []
    for member in members:
        relative = pathlib.PurePosixPath(member.name)
        if relative.is_absolute() or not relative.parts:
            raise SystemExit(f"unsafe evidence path: {member.name!r}")
        if any(part in {"", ".", ".."} for part in relative.parts):
            raise SystemExit(f"unsafe evidence path: {member.name!r}")
        if not member.isfile():
            raise SystemExit(f"unsupported evidence member type: {member.name!r}")
        if member.mode & 0o7000:
            raise SystemExit(f"unsafe evidence mode: {member.name!r}")
        if member.size > 512 * 1024:
            raise SystemExit(f"evidence member exceeds size limit: {member.name!r}")
        target = root.joinpath(*relative.parts).resolve(strict=False)
        if root not in target.parents:
            raise SystemExit(f"evidence path escapes replay root: {member.name!r}")
        validated.append((member, target))

    for member, target in validated:
        target.parent.mkdir(mode=0o700, parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        source = bundle.extractfile(member)
        if source is None:
            raise SystemExit(f"evidence member has no data: {member.name!r}")
        with target.open("xb") as destination:
            destination.write(source.read())

manifest = root / "manifest-r7.sha256"
verified = 0
for line_number, line in enumerate(manifest.read_text().splitlines(), 1):
    fields = line.split("  ", 1)
    if len(fields) != 2:
        raise SystemExit(f"invalid manifest row {line_number}")
    expected, name = fields
    relative = pathlib.PurePosixPath(name)
    if relative.is_absolute() or any(part in {"", ".", ".."} for part in relative.parts):
        raise SystemExit(f"unsafe manifest path on row {line_number}")
    candidate = root.joinpath(*relative.parts).resolve(strict=True)
    if root not in candidate.parents or not candidate.is_file():
        raise SystemExit(f"invalid manifest target on row {line_number}")
    observed = hashlib.sha256(candidate.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
    if observed != expected:
        raise SystemExit(f"manifest digest mismatch on row {line_number}")
    verified += 1
if verified != 86:
    raise SystemExit(f"unexpected verified evidence count: {verified}")
# Row count is not coverage: assert the manifest names exactly the members.
manifest_names = {line.split("  ", 1)[1] for line in manifest.read_text().splitlines() if line.strip()}
member_names = {m.name for m in members} - {"manifest-r7.sha256"}
if manifest_names != member_names:
    raise SystemExit(
        "manifest does not cover every member: "
        f"unmanifested={sorted(member_names - manifest_names)} "
        f"extra={sorted(manifest_names - member_names)}")
print(f"Validated {verified} evidence files beneath {root}")
PY
```

Running any reconstructed fixture requires network-isolated synthetic services
and a fresh generated browser profile. The Linux arm additionally requires a
container runtime and pulls the pinned Playwright image; that image is
deliberately not included here.

Destroy the reconstructed directory after review.
